<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Memorials">Memorials</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Martin_Van_Buren&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: Memorials">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/List_of_memorials_to_Martin_Van_Buren" title="List of memorials to Martin Van Buren">List of memorials to Martin Van Buren</a></div>
<p>Van Buren's home in Kinderhook, New York, which he called Lindenwald, is now the <a href="/wiki/Martin_Van_Buren_National_Historic_Site" title="Martin Van Buren National Historic Site">Martin Van Buren National Historic Site</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-265" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-265">&#91;264&#93;</a></sup> Counties are named for Van Buren in <a href="/wiki/Van_Buren_County,_Michigan" title="Van Buren County, Michigan">Michigan</a>, <a href="/wiki/Van_Buren_County,_Iowa" title="Van Buren County, Iowa">Iowa</a>, <a href="/wiki/Van_Buren_County,_Arkansas" title="Van Buren County, Arkansas">Arkansas</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Van_Buren_County,_Tennessee" title="Van Buren County, Tennessee">Tennessee</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-266" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-266">&#91;265&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Mount_Van_Buren" title="Mount Van Buren">Mount Van Buren</a>, <a href="/wiki/USS_Van_Buren_(1839)" title="USS Van Buren (1839)">USS&#160;<i>Van Buren</i></a>, three state parks and numerous towns were named after him.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Popular_culture">Popular culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Martin_Van_Buren&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: Popular culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Books">Books</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Martin_Van_Buren&amp;action=edit&amp;section=32" title="Edit section: Books">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>In <a href="/wiki/Gore_Vidal" title="Gore Vidal">Gore Vidal</a>'s 1973 novel <i><a href="/wiki/Burr_(novel)" title="Burr (novel)">Burr</a></i>, a major plot theme is an attempt to prevent Van Buren's election as president by proving he is the illegitimate son of <a href="/wiki/Aaron_Burr" title="Aaron Burr">Aaron Burr</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-267" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-267">&#91;266&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Cartoons">Cartoons</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Martin_Van_Buren&amp;action=edit&amp;section=33" title="Edit section: Cartoons">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>During the <a href="/wiki/1988_United_States_presidential_election" title="1988 United States presidential election">1988 presidential campaign</a>, <a href="/wiki/George_H._W._Bush" title="George H. W. Bush">George H. W. Bush</a>, a <a href="/wiki/Yale_University" title="Yale University">Yale University</a> graduate and member of the <a href="/wiki/Skull_and_Bones" title="Skull and Bones">Skull and Bones</a> secret society, was attempting to become the first incumbent vice president to win election to the presidency since Van Buren. In the comic strip <i><a href="/wiki/Doonesbury" title="Doonesbury">Doonesbury</a></i>, artist <a href="/wiki/Garry_Trudeau" title="Garry Trudeau">Garry Trudeau</a> depicted members of Skull and Bones as attempting to rob Van Buren's grave, apparently intending to use the relics in a ritual that would aid Bush in the election.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEWidmer2005170_268-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEWidmer2005170-268">&#91;267&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-269" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-269">&#91;268&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Currency">Currency</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Martin_Van_Buren&amp;action=edit&amp;section=34" title="Edit section: Currency">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Martin Van Buren appeared in the <a href="/wiki/Presidential_dollar_coins" title="Presidential dollar coins">Presidential dollar coins</a> series in 2008.<sup id="cite_ref-270" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-270">&#91;269&#93;</a></sup> The U.S. Mint has also made commemorative silver medals for Van Buren, released for sale on February 1, 2021.<sup id="cite_ref-271" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-271">&#91;270&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-272" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-272">&#91;271&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Film_and_TV">Film and TV</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Martin_Van_Buren&amp;action=edit&amp;section=35" title="Edit section: Film and TV">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Van Buren is portrayed by <a href="/wiki/Nigel_Hawthorne" title="Nigel Hawthorne">Nigel Hawthorne</a> in the 1997 film <a href="/wiki/Amistad_(film)" title="Amistad (film)"><i>Amistad</i></a>. The film depicts the legal battle surrounding the status of slaves who in 1839 rebelled against their transporters on <i><a href="/wiki/La_Amistad" title="La Amistad">La Amistad</a></i> slave ship.<sup id="cite_ref-273" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-273">&#91;272&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On the television show <i><a href="/wiki/Seinfeld" title="Seinfeld">Seinfeld</a></i>, the 1997 episode "<a href="/wiki/The_Van_Buren_Boys" title="The Van Buren Boys">The Van Buren Boys</a>" is about a fictional <a href="/wiki/Street_gang" class="mw-redirect" title="Street gang">street gang</a> that admires Van Buren and bases its rituals and symbols on him, including the hand sign of eight fingers pointing up.<sup id="cite_ref-274" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-274">&#91;273&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In an episode of <a href="/wiki/The_Monkees_(TV_series)" title="The Monkees (TV series)"><i>The Monkees</i></a>, "Dance, Monkee, Dance", a dance instruction studio offers free lessons to anyone who can answer the question, "Who was the eighth President of the United States?" Martin Van Buren, portrayed by Stephen Coit, appears at the studio to claim the prize.<sup id="cite_ref-275" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-275">&#91;274&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Music">Music</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Martin_Van_Buren&amp;action=edit&amp;section=36" title="Edit section: Music">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>At least two bands have incorporated Van Buren as part of their name: the Illinois-based The Van Buren Boys and The Van Burens from Quincy, Massachusetts.<sup id="cite_ref-276" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-276">&#91;275&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-277" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-277">&#91;276&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Charlotte_Dupuy" title="Charlotte Dupuy">Charlotte Dupuy</a>, a slave who worked for Van Buren at <a href="/wiki/Decatur_House" title="Decatur House">Decatur House</a>, while her suit for freedom against <a href="/wiki/Henry_Clay" title="Henry Clay">Henry Clay</a> proceeded</li>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Notes">Notes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Martin_Van_Buren&amp;action=edit&amp;section=38" title="Edit section: Notes">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1011085734">.mw-parser-output .reflist{font-size:90%;margin-bottom:0.5em;list-style-type:decimal}.mw-parser-output .reflist .references{font-size:100%;margin-bottom:0;list-style-type:inherit}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-2{column-width:30em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-3{column-width:25em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns{margin-top:0.3em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns ol{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns li{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-alpha{list-style-type:upper-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-roman{list-style-type:upper-roman}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-alpha{list-style-type:lower-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-greek{list-style-type:lower-greek}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-roman{list-style-type:lower-roman}</style><div class="reflist reflist-lower-alpha">
<div class="mw-references-wrap"><ol class="references">
<li id="cite_note-5"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-5">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">The "mistletoe" nickname was inspired by the fact that mistletoe is a <a href="/wiki/Parasitic_plant" title="Parasitic plant">parasitic plant</a> which attaches itself to a host tree to survive. Comparing Van Buren to mistletoe implied that he advanced politically only through his connection to the "Old Hickory" tree, which represented Andrew Jackson. This idea was common in Whig propaganda pieces, and also seen in political comics such as The Rejected Minister, where Van Buren is shown being carried into office by Jackson and Parscal.</span>
</li>
</ol></div></div>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="References">References</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Martin_Van_Buren&amp;action=edit&amp;section=39" title="Edit section: References">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1011085734"><div class="reflist">
